emergency bill, the “Modification Nos. M02, M03, M04, and M05 to Contract No. DCAM-24-

The legislation will approve Modification Nos. M02, M03, M04, and M05 to Contract No. DCAM-
24-NC-RFP-0003A with Jerome L. Taylor Trucking, Inc. to provide waste collection and disposal

Hunter, Director, DGS, or have your staff contact Eric Njonjo, Interim Chief Procurement Officer,
DGS, at (202) 727-2800.

25 Sec. 2. Pursuant to section 451 of the District of Columbia Home Rule Act, approved

(B) For a contract containing option periods, the contract amount for the base period and
for each option period. If the contract amount for one or more of the option periods
differs from the amount for the base period, provide an explanation of the reason for
the difference:
Base Period: NTE Amount $4,008,190
Council Approval: CA25-0616
Modification No. M01 NTE Amount of
Base Period: $3,763,014 (reduced by $245,176)
Page 2 of 10
Modification No. M02-M04 NTE Amount
(Partial Exercise of Option Year One): $937,087.38
Proposed Modification No. M05 NTE
Amount
(Full Exercise of Option Year One): NTE $4,113,054.36
Explanation of difference from base period:
The Contract provides for two types of fixed unit prices based on the type of service to be
provided: (i) regularly recurring waste collection and disposal services pursuant to the
Contract’s scope of work (“Basic Services”), priced at a fixed unit rate for each type of Basic
Service; and (ii) costs incurred in performing on-call, disposal or supplemental hauling
services against the NTE Owner-Directed Allowance at the request and discretion of the
Department (“Supplemental Services”, together with the Basic Services, the “Services”)
priced at a fixed hourly rate for each type of Supplemental Service. The rates for each option
year include an increase from the fixed unit rates for the preceding year to account for
inflation. Additionally, the NTE amount for Option Year 001 includes compensation for
twelve (12) months (or 52-weeks) of Basic Services and Supplemental Services; the NTE
Amount for the Base Period had included 30-weeks of Basic Services and Supplemental
Services.
(C) The goods or services to be provided, the methods of delivering goods or services, and
any significant program changes reflected in the proposed contract:
Under the Contract for Citywide Waste (trash and recycling) Collection and Disposal Services
(DGS Managed Properties), DCAM-24-NC-RFP-0003A (the “Contract”), the Contractor
provides citywide solid waste (trash and recycling) collection and disposal services, including
all management, tools, supplies, equipment, storage, vehicles, and labor necessary to perform
the required services at various facilities located throughout the District of Columbia, as well
as five (5) collection points in the State of Maryland, requiring front-load, rear-load and roll-
off containers.
The Contract provides for two types of services to be performed by the Contractor: (i) Basic
Services, which include all regularly recurring waste collection and disposal services in
accordance with the established routine service schedule pursuant to the Contract’s scope of
work; and (ii) Supplemental (“Time and Material”) Services, which includes but not limited
to, disposal costs for trailer services (roll-off and compactors), unforeseen emergency
services, relocation of containers, purchase or repair of containers, on-call disposal or
supplemental hauling services performed outside of the routine service schedule (Basic
Services) and performed at the request and discretion of the District. The Contract contains a
Base Period of 30-weeks from March 4, 2024 through September 30, 2024. The Contract also
contains up to four (4) one-year option periods. The underlying Contract was deemed
approved by the Council on March 3, 2024, as CA25-0616 with a NTE amount of $4,008,190.
Modification No. M01 reduced the total firm-fixed fully loaded price for Basic Services from
________________________________________________________________________
Department of General Services 3924 Minnesota Avenue NE, Washington DC 20019
Page 3 of 10
$3,608,190 by $245,176 to $3,363,014 during the Base Period. This Modification No. M01
reduced the Base Period’s NTE amount from $4,008,190 by $245,176, to a total aggregate
NTE amount of $3,763,014.
Modification No. M02 partially exercised Option Year One, extending the contract’s period
of performance from October 1, 2024 to November 18, 2024 with a NTE amount of
$937,087.38. Modification No. M03 extended the contract’s period of performance through
February 24, 2025 with a NTE amount of zero dollars ($0.00). Modification No. M04
extended the contract’s period of performance through April 14, 2025 with a NTE amount of
zero dollars ($0.00). The amount of Modification Nos. M02-M04 were less than $1 million,
thus Council’s approval was not required.
Proposed Modification No. M05 will fully exercise Option Year One, increasing the NTE
amount from $937,087.38 by $3,175,966.98, to a total aggregate NTE amount of
$4,113,054.36. Proposed Modification No. M05 will authorize the Contractor to continue
providing citywide solid waste (trash and recycling) collection and disposal services to
various facilities located throughout the District of Columbia, as well as five (5) collection
points in the State of Maryland, requiring front-load, rear-load and roll-off containers.
Proposed Modification No. M05 will extend the contract’s period of performance through
September 30, 2025. As the combined NTE amount of Modification Nos. M02-M04 and
proposed Modification No. M05 will exceed $1 million, Council approval is required.
(D) The selection process, including the number of offerors, the evaluation criteria, and the
evaluation results, including price, technical or quality, and past performance
components:
On July 13, 2023, the Department issued a Request for Proposals (“RFP”) to engage a
contractor to provide citywide solid waste (trash and recycling) collection and disposal
services, including all management, tools, supplies, equipment, storage, vehicles, and labor
necessary to perform the required services at various facilities located throughout the District
of Columbia, as well as three (3) collection points in the State of Maryland, requiring front-
load, rear-load and roll-off containers. The solicitation was designated for the Open Market.
The pre-proposal conference was held on July 17, 2023, at the Marion S. Barry, Jr. Building
located at 441 4th Street NW, 11th Floor, Room 1012, Washington, DC 20001. The site visit
was held immediately after the pre-proposal conference, at MPD Headquarters located at 300
Indiana Avenue NW, Washington, DC 20001.
The Department issued three (3) addenda to the RFP. Addendum No. 01, issued on July 19,
2023, incorporated the Pre-Proposal Conference Attendance Roster as Exhibit A; and
incorporated Pre-Proposal Conference Agenda as Exhibit B. Addendum No. 02, issued on
July 28, 2023, incorporated Questions and Answers as Exhibit A; deleted in its entirety
Payment and Performance Bond Requirements; deleted in its entirety Attachment A.9,
Payment and Performance Bond; deleted and replaced Section C.5.1.1.2; deleted and replaced
Attachment A.8, Quickbase Vendor Submission Portal Instructions; deleted and replaced

Section 2:
Section C.5.12, Disincentive Fee; and deleted and replaced Section M.3.1.1(b) – Factor A:
Relevant Experience, Past Performance, and Capacity. Addendum No. 03, issued on August
________________________________________________________________________
Department of General Services 3924 Minnesota Avenue NE, Washington DC 20019
Page 4 of 10
7, 2023, incorporated enclosed container breakdown to Question No. 17 from Questions and
Answers issued on July 28, 2023, via Addendum No. 02.
On the proposal due date, August 11, 2023, three (3) firms (“Offerors”) submitted proposals
in a timely manner. One (1) of the three (3) offerors did not comply with the RFP
requirements, and as such were deemed non-responsive.
Technical Evaluation:
The remaining responsive proposals were evaluated by a technical evaluation panel (the
“Panel”) in accordance with the criteria set forth in the RFP. Each proposal was independently
evaluated utilizing a point scale, certain points of which were for the technical evaluation
component as follows: (i) relevant experience, past performance, and capacity; (ii) key
personnel qualifications and staffing strategy; and (iii) project management plan, as discussed
more fully below.
The Panel met on September 7, 2023, to develop a consensus technical score. Prior to
convening the evaluation meeting, each of the Panel members individually completed an
evaluation of the proposal. In doing so, each Panel member rated each responsive Offeror
with respect to specific sub-factors in the three (3) categories.
Pricing Evaluation:
As outlined in the RFP, certain points were available for price. In addition to the technical
scoring, responsive Offerors were assigned price points on a sliding scale, with the lowest
proposed price receiving all of the available points, and the remaining proposal receiving a
portion of the available points relative to its position in the range established by the highest-
and lowest price proposals.
Certified Business Enterprise Preference Points:
In addition to the price and technical scoring, points were available for each Offeror based on
its status as a Certified Business Offer (“CBE”) as determined by DSLBD. The responsive
Offerors were certified and received additional points accordingly.
Contracting Officer’s Independent Evaluation:
The Contracting Officer carefully reviewed the Panel’s consensus technical scoring and the
evaluation process followed by the Panel, including the Panel’s notes, score sheets, and its
final consensus technical evaluation. In addition, the Contracting Officer conducted an
independent assessment of each proposal and exercised her independent judgment in
evaluating the responsive Offerors, after which the Contracting Officer determined that the
Contractor was the highest ranked Offeror.
Pursuant to Title 27 DCMR Section 4706.5, the Contracting Officer may request information
from the contractor and may also consider information available from other sources, including
the general public, in evaluating a prospective contractor’s responsibility. On October 26,
2023, the Department issued a clarification on vendor responsibility letter to the highest-
ranking offeror, Jerome L. Taylor Trucking, Inc., based on a recent article published by a local
Washington, DC newspaper alleging wrongdoing and unethical behavior between Jerome
________________________________________________________________________
Department of General Services 3924 Minnesota Avenue NE, Washington DC 20019
Page 5 of 10
Taylor and personnel at DC Housing Authority (“DCHA”). Upon examination of the
information provided by Jerome L. Taylor Trucking, Inc., the Office of General Counsel
conducted another layer of research and reviewed the Board of Ethics and Government
Accountability’s (“BEGA”) website for any publications on either DCHA or Jerome L. Taylor
Trucking Inc. and found none. Based on Jerome L. Taylor Trucking Inc.’s responses to the
Department’s questions, there is no evidence that Jerome L. Taylor Trucking, Inc. is currently
being investigated or committed any wrongdoing; thus, Jerome L. Taylor Trucking, Inc. is
deemed responsible.
Contract Award:
After reviewing the evaluation Panel’s consensus technical scoring, conducting an
independent assessment in evaluating the responsive Offerors, reviewing the underlying
proposal, documentation and CBE preference points, the Contracting Officer determined that
the Contractor was the highest ranked Offeror. In addition, the Contracting Officer determined
that the Contractor is responsible, and that the Contractor’s proposed pricing is fair and
reasonable. Subsequently, by award memorandum executed December 6, 2023, the
Department awarded Contract No. DCAM-24-NC-RFP-0003A to the Contractor as such
award was determined to be the most advantageous to the District.
(E) A description of any bid protest related to the award of the contract, including whether
the protest was resolved through litigation, withdrawal of the protest by the protestor,
or voluntary corrective action by the District. Include the identity of the protestor, the
grounds alleged in the protest, and any deficiencies identified by the District as a result
of the protest:
On September 8, 2023, DGS was served with a written protest addressed to the Department
and the District of Columbia Contract Appeals Board (“CAB”). The protest, CAB Case No.
is P-1186, was filed with the Contract Appeals Board on September 7, 2023 by WB Waste.
On September 11, 2023, the Department sent out notices of protest to the remaining two (2)
offerors, and further evaluations were halted. On October 3, 2023, WB Waste filed a
Stipulation of Dismissal with CAB to jointly request with the District that the protest be
dismissed with prejudice. Accordingly, CAB granted the motion to dismiss with prejudice.
(F) The background and qualifications of the proposed contractor, including its
organization, financial stability, personnel, and performance on past or current
government or private sector contracts with requirements similar to those of the
proposed contract:
The Contractor is a Washington, DC based full-service waste collection company providing
roll-off, front-load, rear-load hauling services in the government, commercial, and
institutional sectors. The Contractor has provided services to the District’s portfolio of
properties for more than 20 years for contracts that are similar or larger in scope, size and
complexity to the Project. The Department reviewed the Contractor’s staffing plan for the
work contemplated by the Contract and found it sufficient. The Contractor has been
determined responsible in accordance with 27 DCMR 4706.1.

On September 11, 2024, the Department executed Modification No. M02, which partially

with a NTE amount of $937,087.38. On November 18, 2024, the Department executed

(I) The aggregate dollar value of the proposed changes as compared with the amount of the
contract as awarded:
The Contract award contemplated Option Year One valued at a NTE amount of $7,332,808.46
(a total NTE of $6,932,808.46 for basic services and a $400,000 not-to-exceed owner-directed
allowance for supplemental services) pursuant to the Contracting Officer’s determination and
findings. Based on numerous updates and corrections to the Service Facility Locations and
Schedule (e.g., the size and quantity of containers, weekly schedule, number of pulls, etc.),
the Option Year One NTE amount is reduced to $4,113,054.36.
(J) Date on which the contracting officer was notified of the proposed change:
September 5, 2024.
(K) The reason why the proposed change was sent to Council for approval after it is intended
to take effect:
During the development of DGS’s FY 2025 budget, local funds were misallocated, resulting
in an over-allocation to the to the sustainable energy account and under-allocated to the waste
management account. To correct this, a reprogramming of funds was recommended by the
Office of the City Administrator to DGS’ Office of the Chief Financial Officer (OCFO).
Consequently, OCFO retracted its original signed fiscal sufficiency certification following
DGS’ submission of a passive council package in IQ (WF#727371). DGS promptly withdrew
the submission to address the issue.
To avoid lapses in service while addressing the funding misallocation, the Department
exercised a partial option year via Modification Nos. M02-M04, covering the period funded
by the FY 2025 budget. Modification Nos. M02-M04 were not previously submitted to
Council for approval because the total value did not exceed $1 million. Proposed Modification
________________________________________________________________________
Department of General Services 3924 Minnesota Avenue NE, Washington DC 20019
Page 7 of 10
No. M05, which is based on the need to ensure that adequate funding is available for the
Contractor to continue providing the required citywide solid waste (trash and recycling)
collection and disposal services through September 30, 2025, will cause the NTE amount of
the Contract for Option Year One to exceed $1 million; thus, Council approval is required.
(L) The reason for the proposed change:
Modification Nos. M02-M04 and proposed Modification No. M05 will fully exercise Option
Year One with a total NTE amount of $4,113,054.36 and authorize the Contractor to continue
to provide citywide solid waste (trash and recycling) collection and disposal services to
various facilities located throughout the District of Columbia, as well as five (5) collection
points in the State of Maryland, requiring front-load, rear-load and roll-off containers.
(M) The legal, regulatory, or contractual authority for the proposed change:
Article 15 of the Department’s Standard Contract Provisions (Goods and Services) provides
authority for the issuance of modifications. As Modification Nos. M02-M04 and proposed
Modification No. M05 would fully exercise Option Year One and increase the total NTE
amount of the Contract for Option Year One by an amount in excess of $1 million, this
contract action is subject to the requirements of the District of Columbia Home Rule Act,
codified at D.C. Official Code §1-204.51(b). Thus, Council review and approval of the
proposed contract action are required under the District of Columbia Home Rule Act, and the
proposed contract action would be authorized by Council’s enactment of Modification Nos.
M02-M04 and proposed Modification No. M05 to Contract No. DCAM-24-NC-RFP-0003A
Approval and Payment Authorization Emergency Act of 2025, under D.C. Official Code § 2-

Date: January 2, 2025
Subject: Fiscal Sufficiency Review
Modification Nos. M02-M04 and the proposed Modification No. M05 to the Contract for Citywide
Waste Collection and Disposal Services (DGS Managed Properties) (thereafter, the “Services”)
with Jerome L. Taylor Trucking, Inc. (the “Contractor”) has a Not-to-Exceed (“NTE) value of
$4,113,054.36 for Fiscal Year 2025 (a total NTE of $3,713,054.36 for basic services and a
$400,000 not-to-exceed owner-directed allowance for supplemental services). The Contractor will
provide the Services covering the period from October 1, 2024 through September 30, 2025.
As the Agency Fiscal Officer of the Department of General Services (DGS), I hereby acknowledge
that the District’s FY 2025 Budget has sufficient funds for the proposed contract with Jerome L.
Taylor Trucking, Inc. in the amount of $4,113,054.36.

MURIEL BOWSER
MAYOR
February 26, 2025
The Honorable Phil Mendelson
Chairman
Council of the District of Columbia
John A. Wilson Building
1350 Pennsylvania Avenue, NW, Suite 504
Washington, DC 20004
Dear Chairman Mendelson:
Enclosed for consideration and enactment by the Council of the District of Columbia is an
emergency bill, the “Modification Nos. M02, M03, M04, and M05 to Contract No. DCAM-24-
NC-RFP-0003A with Jerome L. Taylor Trucking, Inc. Approval and Payment Authorization
Emergency Act of 2025”, and the accompanying emergency declaration resolution.
The legislation will approve Modification Nos. M02, M03, M04, and M05 to Contract No. DCAM-
24-NC-RFP-0003A with Jerome L. Taylor Trucking, Inc. to provide waste collection and disposal
services, and will authorize payment to Jerome L. Taylor Trucking, Inc. for services received and
to be received under option year one of the contract.
My administration is available to discuss any questions you may have regarding the proposed
legislation. In order to facilitate a response to any questions you may have, please contact Delano
Hunter, Director, DGS, or have your staff contact Eric Njonjo, Interim Chief Procurement Officer,
DGS, at (202) 727-2800.
I urge the Council to take prompt and favorable action on the enclosed legislation.
Sincerely,
Muriel Bowser

25 Sec. 2. Pursuant to section 451 of the District of Columbia Home Rule Act, approved
26 December 24, 1973 (87 Stat. 803; D.C. Official Code§ 1-204.51), and notwithstanding the
27 requirements of section 202(a) of the Procurement Practices Reform Act of 2010, effective April
28 8, 2011 (D.C. Law 18-371; D.C. Official Code§ 2-352.02(a)), the Council approves

COUNCIL CONTRACT SUMMARY
(Tipping)
Modification Nos. M02-M04 and Proposed Modification No. M05 to Fully Exercise Option
Year One of Contract No. DCAM-24-NC-RFP-0003A for Citywide Waste Collection and
Disposal Services (DGS Managed Properties)

Proposed Contractor: Jerome L. Taylor Trucking, Inc.
(“Contractor”)

Not-to-Exceed (“NTE”) Amount
(Partial Exercise of Option Year One): $937,087.38
Proposed Modification No. M05

(Partial Exercise of Option Year One): October 1, 2024 through September 30, 2025
Type of Contract: Firm Fixed Price with a Time and Materials

Source Selection Method: Competitive Request for Proposals
(B) For a contract containing option periods, the contract amount for the base period and
for each option period. If the contract amount for one or more of the option periods
differs from the amount for the base period, provide an explanation of the reason for
the difference:
Base Period: NTE Amount $4,008,190
Council Approval: CA25-0616
Modification No. M01 NTE Amount of
Base Period: $3,763,014 (reduced by $245,176)

Page 2 of 10
Modification No. M02-M04 NTE Amount
(Partial Exercise of Option Year One): $937,087.38
Proposed Modification No. M05 NTE
Amount
(Full Exercise of Option Year One): NTE $4,113,054.36
Explanation of difference from base period:
The Contract provides for two types of fixed unit prices based on the type of service to be
provided: (i) regularly recurring waste collection and disposal services pursuant to the
Contract’s scope of work (“Basic Services”), priced at a fixed unit rate for each type of Basic
Service; and (ii) costs incurred in performing on-call, disposal or supplemental hauling
services against the NTE Owner-Directed Allowance at the request and discretion of the
Department (“Supplemental Services”, together with the Basic Services, the “Services”)
priced at a fixed hourly rate for each type of Supplemental Service. The rates for each option
year include an increase from the fixed unit rates for the preceding year to account for
inflation. Additionally, the NTE amount for Option Year 001 includes compensation for
twelve (12) months (or 52-weeks) of Basic Services and Supplemental Services; the NTE
Amount for the Base Period had included 30-weeks of Basic Services and Supplemental
Services.
(C) The goods or services to be provided, the methods of delivering goods or services, and
any significant program changes reflected in the proposed contract:
Under the Contract for Citywide Waste (trash and recycling) Collection and Disposal Services
(DGS Managed Properties), DCAM-24-NC-RFP-0003A (the “Contract”), the Contractor
provides citywide solid waste (trash and recycling) collection and disposal services, including
all management, tools, supplies, equipment, storage, vehicles, and labor necessary to perform
the required services at various facilities located throughout the District of Columbia, as well
as five (5) collection points in the State of Maryland, requiring front-load, rear-load and roll-
off containers.
The Contract provides for two types of services to be performed by the Contractor: (i) Basic
Services, which include all regularly recurring waste collection and disposal services in
accordance with the established routine service schedule pursuant to the Contract’s scope of
work; and (ii) Supplemental (“Time and Material”) Services, which includes but not limited
to, disposal costs for trailer services (roll-off and compactors), unforeseen emergency
services, relocation of containers, purchase or repair of containers, on-call disposal or
supplemental hauling services performed outside of the routine service schedule (Basic
Services) and performed at the request and discretion of the District. The Contract contains a
Base Period of 30-weeks from March 4, 2024 through September 30, 2024. The Contract also
contains up to four (4) one-year option periods. The underlying Contract was deemed
approved by the Council on March 3, 2024, as CA25-0616 with a NTE amount of $4,008,190.
Modification No. M01 reduced the total firm-fixed fully loaded price for Basic Services from
________________________________________________________________________
Department of General Services 3924 Minnesota Avenue NE, Washington DC 20019

Page 3 of 10
$3,608,190 by $245,176 to $3,363,014 during the Base Period. This Modification No. M01
reduced the Base Period’s NTE amount from $4,008,190 by $245,176, to a total aggregate
NTE amount of $3,763,014.
Modification No. M02 partially exercised Option Year One, extending the contract’s period
of performance from October 1, 2024 to November 18, 2024 with a NTE amount of
$937,087.38. Modification No. M03 extended the contract’s period of performance through
February 24, 2025 with a NTE amount of zero dollars ($0.00). Modification No. M04
extended the contract’s period of performance through April 14, 2025 with a NTE amount of
zero dollars ($0.00). The amount of Modification Nos. M02-M04 were less than $1 million,
thus Council’s approval was not required.
Proposed Modification No. M05 will fully exercise Option Year One, increasing the NTE
amount from $937,087.38 by $3,175,966.98, to a total aggregate NTE amount of
$4,113,054.36. Proposed Modification No. M05 will authorize the Contractor to continue
providing citywide solid waste (trash and recycling) collection and disposal services to
various facilities located throughout the District of Columbia, as well as five (5) collection
points in the State of Maryland, requiring front-load, rear-load and roll-off containers.
Proposed Modification No. M05 will extend the contract’s period of performance through
September 30, 2025. As the combined NTE amount of Modification Nos. M02-M04 and
proposed Modification No. M05 will exceed $1 million, Council approval is required.
(D) The selection process, including the number of offerors, the evaluation criteria, and the
evaluation results, including price, technical or quality, and past performance
components:
On July 13, 2023, the Department issued a Request for Proposals (“RFP”) to engage a
contractor to provide citywide solid waste (trash and recycling) collection and disposal
services, including all management, tools, supplies, equipment, storage, vehicles, and labor
necessary to perform the required services at various facilities located throughout the District
of Columbia, as well as three (3) collection points in the State of Maryland, requiring front-
load, rear-load and roll-off containers. The solicitation was designated for the Open Market.
The pre-proposal conference was held on July 17, 2023, at the Marion S. Barry, Jr. Building
located at 441 4th Street NW, 11th Floor, Room 1012, Washington, DC 20001. The site visit
was held immediately after the pre-proposal conference, at MPD Headquarters located at 300
Indiana Avenue NW, Washington, DC 20001.
The Department issued three (3) addenda to the RFP. Addendum No. 01, issued on July 19,
2023, incorporated the Pre-Proposal Conference Attendance Roster as Exhibit A; and
incorporated Pre-Proposal Conference Agenda as Exhibit B. Addendum No. 02, issued on
July 28, 2023, incorporated Questions and Answers as Exhibit A; deleted in its entirety
Payment and Performance Bond Requirements; deleted in its entirety Attachment A.9,
Payment and Performance Bond; deleted and replaced Section C.5.1.1.2; deleted and replaced
Attachment A.8, Quickbase Vendor Submission Portal Instructions; deleted and replaced
Section C.5.12, Disincentive Fee; and deleted and replaced Section M.3.1.1(b) – Factor A:
Relevant Experience, Past Performance, and Capacity. Addendum No. 03, issued on August
________________________________________________________________________
Department of General Services 3924 Minnesota Avenue NE, Washington DC 20019

Page 4 of 10
7, 2023, incorporated enclosed container breakdown to Question No. 17 from Questions and
Answers issued on July 28, 2023, via Addendum No. 02.
On the proposal due date, August 11, 2023, three (3) firms (“Offerors”) submitted proposals
in a timely manner. One (1) of the three (3) offerors did not comply with the RFP
requirements, and as such were deemed non-responsive.
Technical Evaluation:
The remaining responsive proposals were evaluated by a technical evaluation panel (the
“Panel”) in accordance with the criteria set forth in the RFP. Each proposal was independently
evaluated utilizing a point scale, certain points of which were for the technical evaluation
component as follows: (i) relevant experience, past performance, and capacity; (ii) key
personnel qualifications and staffing strategy; and (iii) project management plan, as discussed
more fully below.
The Panel met on September 7, 2023, to develop a consensus technical score. Prior to
convening the evaluation meeting, each of the Panel members individually completed an
evaluation of the proposal. In doing so, each Panel member rated each responsive Offeror
with respect to specific sub-factors in the three (3) categories.
Pricing Evaluation:
As outlined in the RFP, certain points were available for price. In addition to the technical
scoring, responsive Offerors were assigned price points on a sliding scale, with the lowest
proposed price receiving all of the available points, and the remaining proposal receiving a
portion of the available points relative to its position in the range established by the highest-
and lowest price proposals.
Certified Business Enterprise Preference Points:
In addition to the price and technical scoring, points were available for each Offeror based on
its status as a Certified Business Offer (“CBE”) as determined by DSLBD. The responsive
Offerors were certified and received additional points accordingly.
Contracting Officer’s Independent Evaluation:
The Contracting Officer carefully reviewed the Panel’s consensus technical scoring and the
evaluation process followed by the Panel, including the Panel’s notes, score sheets, and its
final consensus technical evaluation. In addition, the Contracting Officer conducted an
independent assessment of each proposal and exercised her independent judgment in
evaluating the responsive Offerors, after which the Contracting Officer determined that the
Contractor was the highest ranked Offeror.
Pursuant to Title 27 DCMR Section 4706.5, the Contracting Officer may request information
from the contractor and may also consider information available from other sources, including
the general public, in evaluating a prospective contractor’s responsibility. On October 26,
2023, the Department issued a clarification on vendor responsibility letter to the highest-
ranking offeror, Jerome L. Taylor Trucking, Inc., based on a recent article published by a local
Washington, DC newspaper alleging wrongdoing and unethical behavior between Jerome
________________________________________________________________________
Department of General Services 3924 Minnesota Avenue NE, Washington DC 20019

Page 5 of 10
Taylor and personnel at DC Housing Authority (“DCHA”). Upon examination of the
information provided by Jerome L. Taylor Trucking, Inc., the Office of General Counsel
conducted another layer of research and reviewed the Board of Ethics and Government
Accountability’s (“BEGA”) website for any publications on either DCHA or Jerome L. Taylor
Trucking Inc. and found none. Based on Jerome L. Taylor Trucking Inc.’s responses to the
Department’s questions, there is no evidence that Jerome L. Taylor Trucking, Inc. is currently
being investigated or committed any wrongdoing; thus, Jerome L. Taylor Trucking, Inc. is
deemed responsible.
Contract Award:
After reviewing the evaluation Panel’s consensus technical scoring, conducting an
independent assessment in evaluating the responsive Offerors, reviewing the underlying
proposal, documentation and CBE preference points, the Contracting Officer determined that
the Contractor was the highest ranked Offeror. In addition, the Contracting Officer determined
that the Contractor is responsible, and that the Contractor’s proposed pricing is fair and
reasonable. Subsequently, by award memorandum executed December 6, 2023, the
Department awarded Contract No. DCAM-24-NC-RFP-0003A to the Contractor as such
award was determined to be the most advantageous to the District.
(E) A description of any bid protest related to the award of the contract, including whether
the protest was resolved through litigation, withdrawal of the protest by the protestor,
or voluntary corrective action by the District. Include the identity of the protestor, the
grounds alleged in the protest, and any deficiencies identified by the District as a result
of the protest:
On September 8, 2023, DGS was served with a written protest addressed to the Department
and the District of Columbia Contract Appeals Board (“CAB”). The protest, CAB Case No.
is P-1186, was filed with the Contract Appeals Board on September 7, 2023 by WB Waste.
On September 11, 2023, the Department sent out notices of protest to the remaining two (2)
offerors, and further evaluations were halted. On October 3, 2023, WB Waste filed a
Stipulation of Dismissal with CAB to jointly request with the District that the protest be
dismissed with prejudice. Accordingly, CAB granted the motion to dismiss with prejudice.
(F) The background and qualifications of the proposed contractor, including its
organization, financial stability, personnel, and performance on past or current
government or private sector contracts with requirements similar to those of the
proposed contract:
The Contractor is a Washington, DC based full-service waste collection company providing
roll-off, front-load, rear-load hauling services in the government, commercial, and
institutional sectors. The Contractor has provided services to the District’s portfolio of
properties for more than 20 years for contracts that are similar or larger in scope, size and
complexity to the Project. The Department reviewed the Contractor’s staffing plan for the
work contemplated by the Contract and found it sufficient. The Contractor has been
determined responsible in accordance with 27 DCMR 4706.1.

(K) The reason why the proposed change was sent to Council for approval after it is intended
to take effect:
During the development of DGS’s FY 2025 budget, local funds were misallocated, resulting
in an over-allocation to the to the sustainable energy account and under-allocated to the waste
management account. To correct this, a reprogramming of funds was recommended by the
Office of the City Administrator to DGS’ Office of the Chief Financial Officer (OCFO).
Consequently, OCFO retracted its original signed fiscal sufficiency certification following
DGS’ submission of a passive council package in IQ (WF#727371). DGS promptly withdrew
the submission to address the issue.
To avoid lapses in service while addressing the funding misallocation, the Department
exercised a partial option year via Modification Nos. M02-M04, covering the period funded
by the FY 2025 budget. Modification Nos. M02-M04 were not previously submitted to
Council for approval because the total value did not exceed $1 million. Proposed Modification
________________________________________________________________________
Department of General Services 3924 Minnesota Avenue NE, Washington DC 20019

Page 7 of 10
No. M05, which is based on the need to ensure that adequate funding is available for the
Contractor to continue providing the required citywide solid waste (trash and recycling)
collection and disposal services through September 30, 2025, will cause the NTE amount of
the Contract for Option Year One to exceed $1 million; thus, Council approval is required.
(L) The reason for the proposed change:
Modification Nos. M02-M04 and proposed Modification No. M05 will fully exercise Option
Year One with a total NTE amount of $4,113,054.36 and authorize the Contractor to continue
to provide citywide solid waste (trash and recycling) collection and disposal services to
various facilities located throughout the District of Columbia, as well as five (5) collection
points in the State of Maryland, requiring front-load, rear-load and roll-off containers.
(M) The legal, regulatory, or contractual authority for the proposed change:
Article 15 of the Department’s Standard Contract Provisions (Goods and Services) provides
authority for the issuance of modifications. As Modification Nos. M02-M04 and proposed
Modification No. M05 would fully exercise Option Year One and increase the total NTE
amount of the Contract for Option Year One by an amount in excess of $1 million, this
contract action is subject to the requirements of the District of Columbia Home Rule Act,
codified at D.C. Official Code §1-204.51(b). Thus, Council review and approval of the
proposed contract action are required under the District of Columbia Home Rule Act, and the
proposed contract action would be authorized by Council’s enactment of Modification Nos.
M02-M04 and proposed Modification No. M05 to Contract No. DCAM-24-NC-RFP-0003A
Approval and Payment Authorization Emergency Act of 2025, under D.C. Official Code § 2-
352.02.

14. Description of modification (Organized by UCF Section headings, including solicitation/contract subject matter where feasible.)
Contract No. DCAM-24-NC-RFP-0003A Citywide Waste Collection and Disposal Services (DGS Managed Properties)
are hereby modified as follows:
1. In accordance with Section F.2 Option to Extend the Term of the Contract, the District hereby exercises an additional portion of Option Year One (1) for a
fourteen (14)-week period, extending the period through February 24, 2025 at no additional cost ($0.00).
